10:["$","p",null,{"children":["The ",["$","code",null,{"children":"PothosSchemaTypes"}]," contains interfaces for all the various options objects used throughout the\nAPI, along with some other types that plugins may want to extend. Each of the interfaces can be\nextended by a plugin to add new options. Each interface takes a number of relevant generic\nparameters that can be used to make the options more useful. For example, the interface for field\noptions will be passed the shape of the parent, the expected return type, and any arguments."]}]

12:["$","p",null,{"children":["The ",["$","code",null,{"children":"SchemaTypes"}]," type is based on the Generic argument passed to the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"SchemaBuilder"}],", and extended\nwith reasonable defaults. Almost every interface in the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"PothosSchemaTypes"}]," will have access to it\n(look for ",["$","code",null,{"children":"Types extends SchemaTypes"}]," in the generics of almost any interface). 2f:["$","p",null,{"children":["Each of the lifecycle methods above (except ",["$","code",null,{"children":"beforeBuild"}],") expect a return value that matches\ntheir first argument (either a config object, or the resolve/subscribe/resolveType function). 30:["$","p",null,{"children":["Inherited interface fields have a separate output field config for each owning object or interface.\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"onOutputFieldConfig"}]," runs once for each declaration and owner pair in a schema build. On an inherited\nfield, ",["$","code",null,{"children":"parentType"}]," names the owner and ",["$","code",null,{"children":"declaringType"}]," names the interface that originally declared\nthe field. Arguments also have their own configs with the owner's ",["$","code",null,{"children":"parentType"}],". ",["$","code",null,{"children":"kind"}]," and\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"graphqlKind"}]," still describe the field builder that declared the field, so plugin-specific field\noptions keep their original meaning. Resolver and subscriber wrappers receive the owner's config."]}]
31:["$","p",null,{"children":["Plugins that generate shared output types for interface fields must use\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"config.declaringType ?? config.parentType"}]," for the generated type's identity. Plugins\nthat apply type-level behavior should use ",["$","code",null,{"children":"parentType"}]," to look up the owner."]}]
32:["$","p",null,{"children":["Each config object will have the properties expected by the GraphQL for creating the types or fields\n(although some properties like ",["$","code",null,{"children":"resolve"}]," will be added later), but will also include a number of\nPothos specific properties. These properties include ",["$","code",null,{"children":"graphqlKind"}]," to indicate what kind of GraphQL\ntype the config object is for, ",["$","code",null,{"children":"pothosOptions"}],", which contains all the options passed in to the\nschema builder when creating the type or field."]}]
33:["$","p",null,{"children":["If your plugin needs to add additional types or fields to the schema it should do this in the\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"beforeBuild"}]," hook. Any types added to the schema after this, may not be included correctly. Plugins\nshould also account for the fact that a new instance of the plugin will be created each time the\nschema is called, so any types or fields added to the schema should only be applied once (per\nbuilder), even if multiple instances of the plugin are created. To help with this, there is a\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"runUnique"}]," helper on the base plugin class, which accepts a key, and a callback, and will only run\na callback once per builder for the given key, even across multiple ",["$","code",null,{"children":"toSchema"}]," calls."]}]

0 0-.537-.5 5.597 5.597 0 0 0-.807-.444 27.72 27.72 0 0 0-1.007-.436c-.918-.383-1.602-.852-2.053-1.405-.45-.553-.676-1.222-.676-2.005 0-.614.123-1.141.369-1.582.246-.441.58-.804 1.004-1.089a4.494   BasePlugin,
  type PothosOutputFieldConfig,
  type SchemaTypes,
} from '@pothos/core';
import { GraphQLError, type GraphQLFieldResolver } from 'graphql';

declare global {
  namespace PothosSchemaTypes {
    interface Plugins<Types extends SchemaTypes> {
      architectureArgMappers: NameInputPlugin<Types>;
    }
  }
}

class NameInputError extends Error {}

export class NameInputPlugin<Types extends SchemaTypes> extends BasePlugin<Types> {
  override onOutputFieldConfig(fieldConfig: PothosOutputFieldConfig<Types>) {
    if (!fieldConfig.extensions?.normalizeName) {
      return fieldConfig;
    }

    return {
      ...fieldConfig,
      argMappers: [
        ...fieldConfig.argMappers,
        (args: Record<string, unknown>) => {
          if (typeof args.name !== 'string' || !args.name.trim()) {
            throw new NameInputError('Name must not be blank');
          }
          return { ...args, name: args.name.trim() };
        },
        async (args: Record<string, unknown>) => {
          // A real plugin could await a lookup or asynchronous validator here.
          if (args.name === 'reserved') {
            throw new NameInputError('Name is reserved');
          }
          return args;
        },
      ],
    };
  }

  override wrapArgMappers(
    resolver: GraphQLFieldResolver<unknown, Types['Context'], object> | undefined,
    fieldConfig: PothosOutputFieldConfig<Types>,
  ): GraphQLFieldResolver<unknown, Types['Context'], object> | undefined {
    if (!resolver || !fieldConfig.extensions?.normalizeName) {
      return resolver;
    }

    return async (parent, args, context, info) => {
      try {
        return await resolver(parent, args, context, info);
      } catch (error) {
        if (error instanceof NameInputError) {
          throw new GraphQLError(error.message, { extensions: { code: 'BAD_USER_INPUT' } });
        }
        throw error;
      }
    };
  }
}

export const pluginName = 'architectureArgMappers';
SchemaBuilder.registerPlugin(pluginName, NameInputPlugin);
